▝▜██████▀  Opus 5.5 · Claude Pro

❯ We're starting a spike on a new branch, `spike/llamaindex`: a version of the  
  backend that uses                                                             
  LlamaIndex for orchestration, as the brief suggests ("AI/LLM Orchestration:   
  LangChain or LlamaIndex").                                                    
  Create the branch from main before changing anything.                         
                                                                                
  ## Read first                                                                 
  1. `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/HANDOFF.md`. All rules still apply, with one         
  exception, on this branch only:                                               
     the "No LangChain or LlamaIndex" rule (CLAUDE.md, HANDOFF D1) is lifted.   
  Do not touch main.                                                            
  2. `docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md`: the design. It maps every module to its        
  LlamaIndex replacement,                                                       
     lists ten traps found in the LlamaIndex source, and sets out a 7-stage     
  plan. Follow it.                                                              
  3. `docs/llamaindex-spike/sketch.py`: working sketches of TenKNodeParser,     
  BgeEmbedding,                                                                 
     NonZeroBM25Retriever, HybridRetriever and GroundedQueryEngine,             
  smoke-tested against                                                          
     llama-index-core 0.14.25. Start from them, but they are sketches: types,   
  tests and error handling                                                      
     are yours to finish.                                                       
                                                                                
  ## Goal and bar                                                               
  LlamaIndex does the plumbing (Chroma, BM25, RRF fusion, ingestion, LLM        
  providers). Our domain logic                                                  
  stays ours, written as LlamaIndex components: 10-K section detection, the     
  relevance gate, the                                                           
  refusal marker, citation validation (`qa/citations.py` unchanged) and the     
  prompt. The API schema                                                        
  and the frontend must not change.                                             
                                                                                
  Parity bar, measured with `backend/eval/run_eval.py` against the committed    
  `eval/results.md`:                                                            
  hybrid Success@6 and MRR within one question of today on both the TXT (21)    
  and HTML (24) sets,                                                           
  candidate recall no worse, and the gate threshold still separating off-topic  
  from answerable                                                               
  questions. Unanswerable questions must still all be refused.                  
                                                                                
  ## Traps you must handle (details in the design doc)                          
  1. ChromaVectorStore reports similarity as exp(-distance). Convert with 1 +   
  ln(score) before the                                                          
     gate, and pin it with a test.                                              
  2. QueryFusionRetriever: set num_queries=1 (the default 4 makes a hidden LLM  
  call), use_async=False,                                                       
     and pass llm explicitly (otherwise it resolves Settings.llm = OpenAI and   
  crashes without a key).                                                       
  3. Fusion overwrites dense scores with RRF scores in place. Capture dense     
  similarity and both ranks                                                     
     before fusing, because the gate, the API's passage cards and run_eval.py   
  need them.                                                                    
  4. The stock FastEmbedEmbedding drops the bge query prefix. Use our own       
  BaseEmbedding subclass.                                                       
  5. SentenceSplitter: pass the bge tokenizer (the default is tiktoken). Its    
  chunk_size includes the                                                       
     metadata header.                                                           
  6. Use deterministic node ids ({sha256}:{i}) so a retry upserts instead of    
  duplicating.                                                                  
  7. BM25Retriever returns score-0 chunks: drop them. Its filters are fixed at  
  construction, so build                                                        
     a filtered instance per filtered request.                                  
  8. node_to_metadata_dict overwrites a `doc_id` metadata key with the ref      
  document id, so a                                                             
     Document's id_ must be the sha256. Chroma rejects None metadata values.    
  9. Never rely on global `Settings`; inject the LLM and embedder explicitly.   
  10. HybridRetriever subclasses two private methods, so pin llama-index-core   
  exactly in pyproject.                                                         
                                                                                
  ## Stages: one at a time, stop after each one for my review                   
  1. LLM layer: OpenAILike (Groq, Ollama) and Anthropic behind a thin wrapper   
  that maps provider                                                            
     errors to our LLMError messages and reads token usage. Tests use a         
  scripted CustomLLM.                                                           
  2. Storage and dense retrieval: ChromaVectorStore + VectorIndexRetriever.     
  Keep our current chunker,                                                     
     wrapped as a NodeParser, so only one thing changes. Re-ingest; the dense   
  rows must match exactly.                                                      
  3. BM25 and fusion: NonZeroBM25Retriever + HybridRetriever. Explain any shift 
  (RRF counts ranks                                                             
     from 0; BM25 tokenizes differently).                                       
  4. Chunking: TenKNodeParser with SentenceSplitter. Rerun the 200/300/400      
  chunk-size sweep and check                                                    
     with scripts/chunk_stats.py that cuts after "Mr." and "Ltd." have not come 
  back.                                                                         
  5. Query engine: GroundedQueryEngine behind the existing /api/ask. Do not run 
  the answer eval                                                               
     yourself (it needs my Groq key and hours of paced calls). Tell me when     
  it's ready and I'll run                                                       
     `run_answer_eval.py ask`, then `score`.                                    
  6. Ingestion and uploads: IngestionPipeline with a docstore for sha256 dedup. 
  Restore the upload                                                            
     progress bar with an instrumentation handler. Keep the write lock and the  
  BM25 rebuild-and-swap.                                                        
  7. Docs: README, HANDOFF D1, IMPROVEMENTS and CLAUDE.md updated on this       
  branch to describe the                                                        
     LlamaIndex version, plus a results table comparing it with main.           
                                                                                
  After each stage, report: what changed, the test, lint and mypy status, and   
  the eval numbers next to                                                      
  main's. If a stage loses more than one question and you can't explain and fix 
  it, stop and tell me.                                                         
  Don't paper over it: the eval decides whether this spike is worth keeping,    
  not the framework.                                                            
                                                                                
  Before writing code, read the documents and the current modules they replace, 
  then give me a short                                                          
  plan for stage 1 and any place where the design doc is wrong about the        
  current code.

⏺ I've created spike/llamaindex from main and changed nothing else. The
  untracked files (books/, the design doc, the ai-logs) came along untracked and
  I haven't touched them.

  The design doc is wrong about the current code in these places

  1. The two files you pointed me to don't exist. There is no
     docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md and no docs/llamaindex-spike/sketch.py. The design
     is the untracked docs/NovaBridge Q&A on LlamaIndex design.md. sketch.py
     only exists inside ai-logs/2026-11-08-claude-app-llamaindex-design.md
     (written at line 3761, edited later; the doc's inline version leaves out
     BgeEmbedding). I can commit both under the names you used, with the final
     sketch pulled from the log.
  2. It predates the re-ranker. It was checked against 323773e, 7 commits before
     PR #4. The production path is now RRF → FastEmbedReranker on the top 20 →
     top 6 (D14; "reranked" is the default mode in hybrid.py). The sketched
     HybridRetriever cuts the RRF list to top 6, so it would quietly drop the
     re-ranker. ScoredHit also has no rerank_rank/rerank_score, which the
     passage cards now show (d8aa1c9). The fix is to fuse to depth max(k, 20),
     then run our re-ranker as a BaseNodePostprocessor.
  3. The sketched gate reads different numbers. Today the gate takes the best
     dense similarity over all 20 dense candidates, before fusion and re-ranking
     (SearchResult.best_similarity, hybrid.py:117). GroundedQueryEngine only
     looks at the fused top 6, and BM25-only hits there have None. That makes
     the gate stricter, so it can refuse answerable questions. best_similarity
     has to be carried through separately.
  4. A dependency conflict the doc doesn't mention. llama-index-llms-openai-like
     needs llama-index-llms-openai, which requires openai<3. We have
     openai>=3.46.0 (locked at 3.46.0). Using OpenAILike means going down to
     openai 2.x.
  5. Another trap: Anthropic temperature. llama-index-llms-anthropic 0.12.3 only
     leaves temperature out for models on a fixed list
     (ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMP_MODELS). Any other model gets it, and the handoff says
     API 2026-10-21 rejects it on every model. The wrapper has to remove it.
  6. Retries stack. LlamaIndex wraps its own retries (tenacity,
     stop_after_attempt(max_retries)) around the SDK's retries, and the defaults
     are 3 for OpenAI and 10 for Anthropic. Setting max_retries=1 gives one SDK
     retry and no extra tenacity retry, which matches today. I'll pin it with a
     test.
  7. Parity bar. Your bar and the design measure hybrid, but users get reranked.
     I'd hold the reranked rows to the same within-one-question bar.

  Plan for stage 1 (LLM layer)

  - Dependencies (need your OK):
    - llama-index-core==0.14.25, pinned exactly
    - llama-index-llms-openai-like and llama-index-llms-anthropic, pinned to the
      versions I read: 0.8.1 and 0.12.3
    - openai downgraded to <3
    - anthropic>=2.6 already satisfies the integration's >=0.75
  - llm/llamaindex_client.py: LlamaIndexClient implements our existing
    LLMClient.complete(system, messages, max_tokens) -> Completion. Because the
    interface stays the same, QAService, run_answer_eval.py and the API don't
    change in this stage; GroundedQueryEngine takes over in stage 5. It will:
    - pass max_tokens on each call;
    - map the openai/anthropic SDK exceptions that come through LlamaIndex to
      the existing status_error, timeout_error and similar helpers, so messages
      never repeat the provider's text;
    - read finish_reason/stop_reason from reply.raw and keep today's errors for
      truncated, refused, content-filtered and empty replies;
    - take token counts from the usage in reply.raw.
  - Factory: build_llm builds OpenAILike(is_chat_model=True, max_retries=1, 
    timeout=…) or an Anthropic subclass without temperature. Both are passed in
    explicitly, never through Settings.
  - Deleted: openai_compatible.py and anthropic_client.py.
  - Tests: FakeLLM keeps its API (replies, calls) but becomes
    LlamaIndexClient(ScriptedLLM), where ScriptedLLM is a CustomLLM, so the
    existing service and eval tests run through the wrapper. test_llm.py is
    rewritten to raise SDK errors through the real LlamaIndex classes using
    mocked clients. Extra tests cover the Anthropic request having no
    temperature, the retry count, and starting up with no OPENAI_API_KEY.
  - Eval: retrieval doesn't change, so I'll rerun run_eval.py to show the
    numbers are identical. I'll leave llm_smoke.py against the real providers to
    you, since it needs your keys.
  - mypy: mypy strict may need casts around reply.raw; any per-module relaxation
    will be listed in the report.

  Before I start, I need three answers:
  1. Do you approve the dependencies, including downgrading openai to 2.x?
  2. Should I commit the design doc and the extracted sketch under the names you
     gave?
  3. Should the parity bar include the reranked rows?

        6 +                                                              
        7 +A LlamaIndex version is feasible: about two thirds of today's 
          +backend becomes library calls, and the parts that make the sys
          +tem trustworthy survive as LlamaIndex components rather than d
          +isappearing. The recommended shape is **LlamaIndex for the plu
          +mbing, our domain logic plugged in as LlamaIndex components**.
        8 +

          +pper, RRF, the indexing header, the ingestion loop, the LLM ad
          +apters and the fake LLM and embedder used in tests.           
       10 +- **Kept, rewritten as components:** 10-K section detection (a
          + custom `NodeParser`), the relevance gate and citation validat
          +ion (a `CustomQueryEngine`), and the prompt.                  
       11 +- **Not used, on purpose:** `CitationQueryEngine`, `Similarity
          +Postprocessor` and the default `QueryFusionRetriever` settings
          +. Each one silently breaks a guarantee we have today (see Trap
          +s).

          +beddings-fastembed` 0.7.0, on the repo as of commit `323773e`

          +n against the eval yet; the migration plan says how to prove p

  ⎿  Updated docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md (+11 -1)
      1  # NovaBridge Q&A on LlamaIndex: design
      2  
      3 -> Committed on `spike/llamaindex` as found. Its claims were chec
        -ked against `323773e`; see the stage 1 notes in the spike log fo
        -r where the code has moved since (the re-ranker, the gate input,
        - the `openai<3` pin).                                           
      3 +> Committed on `spike/llamaindex` as found. Its claims were chec
        +ked against `323773e`; "Corrections found before stage 1" at the
        + end lists where the code has moved since.                      
      4  
      5  Nov 8, 2026 · @Ivan
      6  
     ...
      225  - *"What did the source reading cost?"* About an hour, and it 
           found ten behaviour changes. That is the argument for reading 
           what a framework does before adopting it.
      226  
      227  The prep doc's numbers are from the 3-filing corpus; the repo 
           now has 6 filings and 45 answerable questions, so refresh them
            from `eval/results.md` before the interview.
      228 +                                                              
      229 +## Corrections found before stage 1                           
      230 +                                                              
      231 +Checked on Nov 9, 2026 against `main` at `83146fa`, before any
          + spike code was written.                                      
      232 +                                                              
      233 +1. **The re-ranker is missing.** PR #4 added a cross-encoder a
          +fter RRF (HANDOFF D14), so production retrieval is RRF to dept
          +h `max(k, 20)`, then `FastEmbedReranker`, then top k. The sket
          +ched `HybridRetriever` cuts to top k at fusion, which would dr
          +op it, and `ScoredHit` has no `rerank_rank` / `rerank_score` f
          +or the passage cards. Parity is measured on the reranked rows 
          +as well as hybrid.                                            
      234 +2. **The gate reads a different set.** Today it checks the bes
          +t dense similarity over all dense candidates, before fusion an
          +d re-ranking (`SearchResult.best_similarity`). The sketched `G
          +roundedQueryEngine` takes the max over the fused top k only, w
          +hich is stricter. The retriever must report `best_similarity` 
          +separately.                                                   
      235 +3. **`openai<3`.** `llama-index-llms-openai` 0.8.3 (needed by 
          +`-openai-like`) requires `openai<3`; `main` locks 3.46.0. The 
          +spike downgrades `openai` (approved by Ivan).                 
      236 +4. **Anthropic `temperature`.** `llama-index-llms-anthropic` 0
          +.12.3 omits `temperature` only for models in its hard-coded `A
          +NTHROPIC_NO_TEMP_MODELS`; API version 2026-10-21 rejects it on
          + every model, so our wrapper removes it.                      
      237 +5. **Retries stack.** LlamaIndex wraps SDK retries in its own 
          +tenacity retry (`stop_after_attempt(max_retries)`), with defau
          +lts of 3 (OpenAI) and 10 (Anthropic). `max_retries=1` gives to
          +day's behaviour: one SDK retry, no tenacity retry.

       1 """Our `LLMClient` interface on top of a LlamaIndex `LLM`.
       2 
       3 LlamaIndex makes the provider call. This wrapper keeps the behav
         iour the rest of the app relies on:
       4 provider errors become `LLMError` messages that are safe to show
         , replies that arrive as HTTP 200
       5 but cannot be used (cut off, refused, empty) raise instead of be
         ing returned, and token usage is
       6 reported when the provider gives it.
       7 """

❯ As a rule during development                                                  
  Delete what LlamaIndex replaces                                               
                                                                                
  This branch exists to show the contrast with main, so delete replaced code    
  instead of keeping it                                                         
  alongside: no compatibility shims, no unused modules, no "legacy" folders.    
  Main keeps the                                                                
  hand-written version.                                                         
  - Delete when its stage lands: retrieval/dense.py, sparse.py, fusion.py,      
  hybrid.py;                                                                    
    ingestion/pipeline.py; llm/openai_compatible.py, anthropic_client.py,       
  fake.py. Delete                                                               
    ingestion/chunking.py in stage 4 only: stage 2 keeps it on purpose, so each 
  stage changes one thing.                                                      
  - Rewrite as LlamaIndex components: retrieval/embedder.py, llm/base.py +      
  factory.py, qa/service.py,                                                    
    and the ingestion part of ingestion/uploads.py.                             
  - Do not touch: ingestion/sections.py, qa/citations.py, qa/prompt.py, the     
  loaders, api/, eval/,                                                         
    frontend/.                                                                  
  - Delete the unit tests for deleted modules too, but first move each          
  behaviour they guarded (zero-score                                            
    BM25 hits dropped, renumbered citations, crash-safe re-ingest, gate         
  threshold) to a test against                                                  
    the new component. Coverage of behaviour must not drop, only tests of code  
  that no longer exists.                                                        
  - In stage 7, add a "Main vs spike" table to the docs: lines of code we own   
  per area (from                                                                
    git diff --stat main...spike/llamaindex and wc -l), dependencies added, and 
  eval numbers side                                                             
    by side.

⏺ Another trap: the LlamaIndex Anthropic class rejects any model id that isn't
  on its hard-coded list, so a newly released Claude model would fail on every
  call. Checking where that lookup happens:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The chat callback reads metadata on every call, and that's where the lookup
  happens. I'll override metadata so an unlisted model id falls back to
  defaults, since we never use the context window.

⏺ I've stopped partway through stage 1 because of a blocker. LlamaIndex's
  Anthropic integration doesn't work with our Anthropic SDK; the
  OpenAI-compatible path (Groq, Ollama) works.

  The blocker

  llama-index-llms-anthropic 0.12.3, the latest release, builds each message by
  calling anthropic.types.MessageParam(...) (utils.py:397). That only works in
  Anthropic SDK 1.x, where MessageParam is a dictionary type. From SDK 2.0 it's
  a Union, so with our locked 2.6.0 every Anthropic call fails with TypeError: 
  Cannot instantiate typing.Union. That's the 16 failing tests: all are
  Anthropic tests, and they fail on the real LlamaIndex code path.

  The ways out:

  - A. Downgrade the SDK to 1.17.0, the last 1.x release. This changes more than
    the library: 1.17 sends the API version header 2023-06-01 instead of
    2026-10-21. I can't check whether current models and our no-temperature rule
    still behave the same on the old version without your key, so it would need
    a smoke test from you.
  - B. Keep SDK 2.6 and override chat in our Anthropic subclass, converting
    messages ourselves (about 15 lines, mostly the old adapter's request code).
    The existing tests already cover it. The catch is that LlamaIndex then
    contributes almost nothing to the Anthropic path, which counts against the
    framework.
  - C. Support only the OpenAI-compatible provider on this branch and record
    Anthropic as unsupported by LlamaIndex with SDK 2.x.

  I'd go with B. It keeps the API version main was tested on, and the gap is
  itself a result of the spike. Which do you want?

  Two more defaults that would have broken things

  - Unknown Claude model ids are rejected. The Anthropic integration looks up
    the model in its own hard-coded list on every call and raises ValueError for
    anything it doesn't know. With model ids coming from .env, a newly released
    model would fail. I overrode metadata to fall back to defaults, since
    nothing here uses the context window.
  - Responses with no choices crash. The OpenAI integration reads choices[0]
    without checking, so a reply with no choices crashes with IndexError. The
    wrapper now turns that into our "returned an empty response" error, and a
    test covers it.

  What's done so far (not committed)

  - Committed earlier (4eacf9d): the design doc as docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md,
    plus a corrections section listing where it no longer matches main. Also
    sketch.py, rebuilt from the session log by replaying the three edits, the
    Claude-app design logs, and a new line in ai-logs/README.md.
  - Dependencies: llama-index-core==0.14.25, llama-index-llms-openai-like==0.8.1
    and llama-index-llms-anthropic==0.12.3, with openai now >=2,<3 (2.54.0
    installed).
    - The Anthropic integration requires anthropic[bedrock,vertex], which pulls
      in boto3 and the Google auth packages.
    - Through those, python-dateutil went down from 4.0.0 to 2.9.0.
  - llm/llamaindex_client.py: LlamaIndexClient keeps our complete() interface.
    It sets max_tokens on each call, maps SDK errors to our messages, raises on
    cut-off, refused or empty replies, and reads token usage from the raw
    response.
  - factory.py: builds OpenAILike with is_chat_model=True and max_retries=1,
    plus AnthropicWithoutTemperature.
  - Deleted, following your new rule: openai_compatible.py and
    anthropic_client.py.
    - llm/fake.py is gone from src. The scripted CustomLLM now lives in
      tests/scripted_llm.py, behind the same wrapper as the real providers.
    - llm/base.py keeps LLMError and the LLMClient interface for now, because
      QAService calls it until stage 5 replaces it.
  - Tests: every behaviour from the old LLM tests is still covered, now through
    the real LlamaIndex classes. New tests cover max_tokens per call, LlamaIndex
    adding no retry of its own on top of the SDK's, and an empty key not
    picking up OPENAI_API_KEY.
  - Checks: pytest 304 passed, 16 failed (all Anthropic). Ruff, format and mypy
    are clean; mypy needed one override because llama_index.llms.openai_like
    ships no type information.
  - Eval: I haven't run run_eval.py yet. Retrieval is untouched, so the numbers
    should match main; I'll run it once the stage is finished, before
    committing.

  ⎿  Updated docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md (+6 -2)
      233  1. **The re-ranker is missing.** PR #4 added a cross-encoder a
           fter RRF (HANDOFF D14), so production retrieval is RRF to dept
           h `max(k, 20)`, then `FastEmbedReranker`, then top k. The sket
           ched `HybridRetriever` cuts to top k at fusion, which would dr
           op it, and `ScoredHit` has no `rerank_rank` / `rerank_score` f
           or the passage cards. Parity is measured on the reranked rows 
           as well as hybrid.
      234  2. **The gate reads a different set.** Today it checks the bes
           t dense similarity over all dense candidates, before fusion an
           d re-ranking (`SearchResult.best_similarity`). The sketched `G
           roundedQueryEngine` takes the max over the fused top k only, w
           hich is stricter. The retriever must report `best_similarity` 
           separately.
      235  3. **`openai<3`.** `llama-index-llms-openai` 0.8.3 (needed by 
           `-openai-like`) requires `openai<3`; `main` locks 3.46.0. The 
           spike downgrades `openai` (approved by Ivan).
      236 -4. **Anthropic `temperature`.** `llama-index-llms-anthropic` 0
          -.12.3 omits `temperature` only for models in its hard-coded `A
          -NTHROPIC_NO_TEMP_MODELS`; API version 2026-10-21 rejects it on
          - every model, so our wrapper removes it.                      
      237 -5. **Retries stack.** LlamaIndex wraps SDK retries in its own 
          -tenacity retry (`stop_after_attempt(max_retries)`), with defau
          -lts of 3 (OpenAI) and 10 (Anthropic). `max_retries=1` gives to
          -day's behaviour: one SDK retry, no tenacity retry.            
      236 +4. **Retries stack.** LlamaIndex wraps OpenAI SDK retries in i
          +ts own tenacity retry (`stop_after_attempt(max_retries)`, defa
          +ult 3). `max_retries=1` gives today's behaviour: one SDK retry
          +, no tenacity retry. A test pins it.                          
      237 +                                                              
      238 +Found while building stage 1 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      239 +                                                              
      240 +5. **No working Anthropic integration.** `llama-index-llms-ant
          +hropic` 0.12.3, the latest release, builds requests with `anth
          +ropic.types.MessageParam(...)`. That is a TypedDict up to SDK 
          +1.17 and a `Union` from SDK 2.0, so with SDK 2.x every call ra
          +ises `TypeError: Cannot instantiate typing.Union`. Downgrading
          + to SDK 1.17 would also change the API version header from `20
          +26-10-21` to `2023-06-01`. Ivan's decision: this branch suppor
          +ts `LLM_PROVIDER=openai_compatible` only (Groq, Ollama, OpenAI
          +), and the comparison with main records Anthropic as lost. Two
          + more defaults in that integration would also have needed over
          +riding: it sends `temperature` unless the model id is in its `
          +ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMP_MODELS` (API 2026-10-21 rejects it on every 
          +model), and on every call it raises `ValueError` for a model i
          +d missing from its context-window table, because the chat call
          +back reads `metadata`.                                        
      241 +6. **Empty `choices`.** The OpenAI integration reads `response
          +.choices[0]` without checking, so a reply with no choices rais
          +es `IndexError`. Our wrapper maps it to the existing "returned
          + an empty response" error.

⏺ Stage 1 is done and committed as 2c1f7e7, with Anthropic dropped on this
  branch as you chose. I've saved your eval preference: from now on I'll only
  run run_eval.py when retrieval or chunking actually changes, and ask first if
  I'm unsure.

  What changed

  - LLM calls go through LlamaIndex's OpenAILike (Groq, Ollama, OpenAI). A new
    wrapper, llm/llamaindex_client.py, keeps the old complete() interface, so
    QAService, the API and both eval scripts are unchanged. It keeps today's
    behaviour:
    - provider errors become the same safe LLMError messages;
    - cut-off, filtered or empty replies still raise an error instead of being
      returned;
    - token usage is still reported;
    - max_tokens is set per call, with one SDK retry and no extra LlamaIndex
      retry on top;
    - an empty key never picks up OPENAI_API_KEY.
  - Anthropic is removed from config.py, .env.example, llm_smoke.py and the
    dependencies. LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic now fails at startup. Your .env already
    uses openai_compatible.
  - Deleted: openai_compatible.py, anthropic_client.py and llm/fake.py. The fake
    is now a LlamaIndex CustomLLM in tests/scripted_llm.py, behind the same
    wrapper as the real provider.
  - Dependencies: added llama-index-core==0.14.25 and
    llama-index-llms-openai-like==0.8.1. openai went from 3.46.0 to 2.54.0, and
    anthropic was removed. No other package changed version, though 26 new
    packages arrived with LlamaIndex (nltk, tiktoken, SQLAlchemy and others).
  - Docs: docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md now records the Anthropic incompatibility,
    the two other Anthropic defaults that would have needed overriding, and the
    empty-choices crash.

  Checks

  - ruff, format and mypy are clean. mypy needed one override because
    llama_index.llms.openai_like ships no type information.
  - pytest: 303 passed. That's 15 fewer than main, all Anthropic-only tests.
    Every OpenAI-compatible behaviour is still tested, now through the real
    LlamaIndex code, plus new tests for max_tokens per call and the retry count.

⏺ Chroma's similarity scores come out as exp(-distance), and its writes use add
  rather than upsert; it also turns None metadata into empty strings, and
  get_nodes([]) returns every node. I'm reading how the nodes get serialized
  next.

  last. Next I'm checking which code in pipeline.py calls the chunk helpers.

  into BaseEmbedding subclasses, and wrapping the chunker as a NodeParser. Dense
  search will go through VectorIndexRetriever, with scores converted back via 1
  + ln(score).

           1.17 and a `Union` from SDK 2.0, so with SDK 2.x every call ra
           ises `TypeError: Cannot instantiate typing.Union`. Downgrading
            to SDK 1.17 would also change the API version header from `20
           26-10-21` to `2023-06-01`. Ivan's decision: this branch suppor
           ts `LLM_PROVIDER=openai_compatible` only (Groq, Ollama, OpenAI
           ), and the comparison with main records Anthropic as lost. Two
            more defaults in that integration would also have needed over
           riding: it sends `temperature` unless the model id is in its `
           ANTHROPIC_NO_TEMP_MODELS` (API 2026-10-21 rejects it on every 
           model), and on every call it raises `ValueError` for a model i
           d missing from its context-window table, because the chat call
           back reads `metadata`.
      241  6. **Empty `choices`.** The OpenAI integration reads `response
           .choices[0]` without checking, so a reply with no choices rais
           es `IndexError`. Our wrapper maps it to the existing "returned
            an empty response" error.
      242 +                                                              
      243 +Found while building stage 2 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      244 +                                                              
      245 +7. **`ChromaVectorStore.add` uses Chroma's `add`, not `upsert`
          +.** A row whose id already exists is kept as it was. With dete
          +rministic `{sha256}:{i}` ids and node 0 written last, a retry 
          +after a crash still adds exactly what is missing; a test crash
          +es the second batch and retries.                              
      246 +8. **`get_nodes(node_ids=[])` returns every node**, because an
          + empty list becomes "no filter". `ChunkStore.get` returns earl
          +y on an empty list.                                           
      247 +9. **`NodeParser` post-processing.** By default it merges the 
          +parent document's metadata into each node (putting the documen
          +t's keys first) and overwrites `start_char_idx` with its own `
          +text.find()`. Our parser sets `include_metadata=False` and kee
          +ps offsets in its own `char_start` / `char_end` metadata.     
      248 +10. **Indexes are not interchangeable with main.** Nodes are s
          +tored as JSON in `_node_content`. `ChunkStore` refuses an inde
          +x written on main (`IndexFormatError`), because ingestion woul
          +d otherwise skip its documents as already indexed. Each branch
          + needs its own `data/index`.                                  
      249 +11. **`run_eval.py` read the store directly** for the gate tab
          +le. It now asks the retriever for `best_similarity` in dense m
          +ode: the value the gate checks.                               
      250 +                                                              

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Stage 2 is done and committed as a370d52. Chunks are now stored in
  LlamaIndex's ChromaVectorStore and dense search goes through
  VectorIndexRetriever, with retrieval results identical to main.

  What changed

  - retrieval/dense.py is deleted. retrieval/store.py replaces it, keeping only
    what LlamaIndex doesn't provide:
    - opening the store with the cosine setting and the embedding-model check;
    - mapping between chunks and LlamaIndex nodes; a test pins that the embedded
      text equals indexed_text();
    - converting the store's score back with 1 + ln(score) (trap 1), also pinned
      by a test;
    - the document list and counts the API and uploads use.
  - Dense search runs through
    VectorStoreIndex.from_vector_store(...).as_retriever(), with the embedding
    model passed in explicitly rather than read from global Settings.
  - Embedders are now LlamaIndex BaseEmbedding subclasses that still count
    tokens for the chunker. The bge query instruction is kept (trap 4), and a
    test checks it.
  - Chunker is unchanged, wrapped as a NodeParser. Upload progress and the "node
    0 written last" crash safety are kept.
  - Wiring-only edits in eval/, api/ and scripts/, as agreed. One of them fixes
    something I missed: run_eval.py read the store directly for the gate table.
    Because of that, my first eval run crashed after about 90 seconds. Sorry for
    the wasted run.
  - Dependency added: llama-index-vector-stores-chroma==0.6.0. No other package
    version changed.

  Things LlamaIndex does by default that needed handling (all now in the design 
  doc)

  - ChromaVectorStore writes with Chroma's add, which keeps existing rows
    instead of overwriting them. A retried ingest is still safe because ids are
    deterministic; a test crashes a write mid-way and retries.
  - get_nodes([]) returns every node in the store, so the store returns early on
    an empty list.
  - NodeParser by default merges the document's metadata into each chunk and
    recomputes each chunk's character offsets. I turned the merge off; offsets
    come from our own chunker.
  - An index built on main can't be read on this branch. The store now refuses
    it with "Delete it and run scripts/ingest.py again". I checked this against
    your local data/index, which I left untouched because main still uses it.

  Checks

  ruff, format and mypy are clean. pytest: 314 passed. Coverage moved, it didn't
  drop: every behaviour tested in test_dense.py is now tested in test_store.py,
  with new tests for the score conversion, filters, embedding text, get([]) and
  refusing an old index.

  Eval (one run, as this stage changes retrieval)

  ⎿  Updated docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md (+14 -0)
      248  10. **Indexes are not interchangeable with main.** Nodes are s
           tored as JSON in `_node_content`. `ChunkStore` refuses an inde
           x written on main (`IndexFormatError`), because ingestion woul
           d otherwise skip its documents as already indexed. Each branch
            needs its own `data/index`.
      249  11. **`run_eval.py` read the store directly** for the gate tab
           le. It now asks the retriever for `best_similarity` in dense m
           ode: the value the gate checks.
      250  
      251 +                                                              
      252 +Found while building stage 3 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      253 +                                                              
      254 +12. **`pystemmer<3`.** `llama-index-retrievers-bm25` 0.8.0 pin
          +s `pystemmer<3`; `main` locks 3.1.0. The spike keeps 3.1.0 thr
          +ough a uv `override-dependencies` entry (approved by Ivan). Th
          +e BM25 tests cover the stemmer.                               
      255 +13. **`QueryFusionRetriever` defaults.** `num_queries` default
          +s to 4, which makes one LLM call per question to write three m
          +ore queries. When no `llm` is passed it falls back to `Setting
          +s.llm`, which is OpenAI. The spike sets `num_queries=1` and pa
          +sses an LLM that raises if called.                            
      256 +14. **Fusion overwrites scores in place.** After RRF, a hit's 
          +dense similarity and its rank in each list are lost, and the g
          +ate, the passage cards and the eval need all three. `RankKeepi
          +ngFusionRetriever` records them before fusing. It calls two pr
          +ivate methods, which is one more reason `llama-index-core` is 
          +pinned exactly.                                               
      257 +15. **RRF constant.** LlamaIndex counts ranks from 0 and fixes
          + k at 60, so a hit scores `1/(60+r)`; `main` scored `1/(61+r)`
          +. Order is unchanged on the eval set. `RRF_K` is now validated
          + to be 60, because the setting can no longer change anything. 
      258 +16. **`BM25Retriever` edge cases.**                           
      259 +    - It returns hits with a score of 0, including chunks mask
          +ed out by a filter. They are dropped, because RRF would still 
          +score them by rank.                                           
      260 +    - It raises `ValueError` on an empty corpus, and again whe
          +n a filter leaves no node. Both cases are checked against Chro
          +ma first.                                                     
      261 +    - Filters are fixed when the retriever is built, so each f
          +iltered request uses a copy that shares the index.            
      262 +    - When the index is built in memory rather than loaded, `b
          +m25.corpus` is `None`, and a filtered copy then ignores the fi
          +lter. The subclass fills it in.                               
      263 +                                                              
      264 +Stage 3 eval: every recall, MRR, candidate-recall, miss and ga
          +te row in `eval/results.md` is identical to `main`. Only the l
          +atencies differ, within run-to-run noise.

⏺ Stage 3 (BM25 and fusion) is done and committed as fad5ef7. The retrieval eval
  matches main on every quality row; only the latencies moved.

  Eval against main (one run, about 4.5 min)
  - Success@6, MRR, candidate recall, misses and the gate threshold table are
    identical to main on TXT and HTML, for the reranked, hybrid, dense and
    sparse rows.
  - Latency changes are noise: hybrid 7–8 ms → 8 ms median, reranked 390 → 393
    ms at 300 tokens.
  - I restored the committed results.md because only the timings had changed.
  - The RRF formula differs slightly: LlamaIndex scores a hit 1/(60+r) with
    ranks counted from 0, where main uses 1/(61+r). It doesn't reorder any of
    the eval results.

  What changed
  - retrieval/bm25.py: LlamaIndex's BM25Retriever, with three fixes:
    - It drops hits that score 0. Without this, chunks a filter had removed came
      back with score 0 and still got RRF points for their rank.
    - Filtered requests get a cheap copy that shares the index.
    - It fills in the corpus when the index is built in memory. Otherwise
      filtered copies silently ignored the filter.
  - retrieval/fusion_retriever.py: LlamaIndex's QueryFusionRetriever with
    num_queries=1 and an LLM that raises if called, so it never makes extra LLM
    calls. It saves each passage's dense and BM25 ranks, its dense similarity,
    and the best dense similarity before fusion replaces the scores. The gate,
    the passage cards and the eval need these.
  - retrieval/search.py replaces hybrid.py. The relevance gate still reads the
    best dense similarity from all candidates, before cutting to top k, and the
    re-ranker runs after fusion as on main.
  - Deleted: hybrid.py, sparse.py, fusion.py and their tests. What those tests
    checked is now covered by test_bm25.py (14 tests), test_fusion_retriever.py
    (11) and test_search.py.
  - Config and dependencies:
    - RRF_K must now be 60, because LlamaIndex's fusion doesn't let us change
      it.
    - Added llama-index-retrievers-bm25==0.8.0.
    - Kept PyStemmer at 3.1.0 through the uv override you approved; no other
      locked versions changed.
  - Design doc: findings 12–16 added to docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md.

  Checks: ruff, format and mypy are clean, and all 328 tests pass.

  I left books/ uncommitted because it isn't from this work. I'll wait for your
  go-ahead before stage 4: TenKNodeParser, deleting chunking.py, and the
  200/300/400-token chunk sweep.

      262      - When the index is built in memory rather than loaded, `b
           m25.corpus` is `None`, and a filtered copy then ignores the fi
           lter. The subclass fills it in.
      263  
      264  Stage 3 eval: every recall, MRR, candidate-recall, miss and ga
           te row in `eval/results.md` is identical to `main`. Only the l
           atencies differ, within run-to-run noise.
      265 +                                                              
      266 +Found while building stage 4 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      267 +                                                              
      268 +17. **`SentenceSplitter`'s sentence tokenizer knows no abbrevi
          +ations.** It builds `PunktSentenceTokenizer()` with no trainin
          +g data, so it cuts after "Mr.", "Ltd.", "Inc.", "Co.", "No.", 
          +"Dr." and "U.S.", even before a lowercase word. With the stock
          + splitter at 400 tokens, 39 of 1,035 chunks in the corpus ende
          +d on one of them, for example "…From 1985 to 1993, Mr." and "…
          +(Case No.". `TenKNodeParser` passes our boundary rule (blank l
          +ines, plus the abbreviation guard from `chunking.py`) as `chun
          +king_tokenizer_fn`. SentenceSplitter still does the packing, t
          +he overlap and the fallbacks. With our rule, the chunks that e
          +nd on an abbreviation in the corpus all end a sentence ("…Form
          + 10-K.", "…outside the U.S."). This also means NLTK's `punkt_t
          +ab` data is never loaded.                                     
      269 +18. **The fallback splits at commas.** A sentence too long for
          + one window is split by `[^,.;]+[,.;]?` before spaces, which c
          +uts inside numbers such as "1,234" in tables. `secondary_chunk
          +ing_regex` now splits at line breaks (table rows) first, as `m
          +ain` did.                                                     
      270 +19. **Chunk size stays text-only.** The sketch used `split_tex
          +t_metadata_aware` with `chunk_size` near 460, so the size woul
          +d include the "title · section" header. The spike calls `split
          +_text`, so `CHUNK_TOKENS` keeps its meaning on `main` and the 
          +200/300/400 sweep compares like for like. At 400 tokens of tex
          +t plus a header of 10–30, the indexed string is still under bg
          +e's 512.                                                      
      271 +                                                              
      272 +Stage 4 eval: chunk counts are 2,335 / 1,406 / 1,023, against 
          +2,363 / 1,434 / 1,028 on `main`. R@6 for reranked and hybrid i
          +s identical to `main` at all three sizes, on both question set
          +s.                                                            
      273 +- At 400 (the default), every quality row is unchanged.       
      274 +- At 300, TXT dense R@20 rises from 0.86 to 0.90 and either-re
          +triever recall from 0.90 to 0.95. Hybrid MRR drops from 0.552 
          +to 0.549.                                                     
      275 +- At 200, TXT hybrid MRR rises from 0.503 to 0.537 (m07 moves 
          +from rank 9 to 7). HTM dense loses one question at R@1 (MRR 0.
          +608 to 0.589).                                                
      276 +                                                              
      277 +The relevance gate table is unchanged.                        

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Stage 4 (chunking) is done and committed as fa39ce3. Retrieval holds parity
  with main at 200, 300 and 400 tokens, and the cuts after "Mr." and "Ltd." have
  not come back.

  Two LlamaIndex defaults that don't work for filings
  1. Sentence splitting: SentenceSplitter uses NLTK's Punkt with no training
     data, so it knows no abbreviations. It cuts after "Mr.", "Ltd.", "Inc.",
     "Co.", "No." and "U.S.", even when a lowercase word follows. With it, 39 of
     1,035 chunks in our corpus ended on one of these, for example "…From 1985
     to 1993, Mr." and "…(Case No.".
     - Fix: TenKNodeParser gives the splitter our own sentence rule from main.
       LlamaIndex still does the packing, overlap and fallbacks. This also means
       NLTK's punkt_tab data is never loaded.
  2. Long-sentence fallback: by default a sentence too long for one chunk is
     split at commas, which cuts inside numbers like "1,234" in tables.
     - Fix: it now splits at line breaks (table rows) first, as main did.

  What changed
  - ingestion/tenk_parser.py (155 lines) replaces chunking.py (124 lines,
    deleted) and the stage-2 wrapper in pipeline.py (161 → 89 lines). It finds
    our Item N. sections and runs SentenceSplitter inside each one, counting
    tokens with the embedder's own tokenizer instead of tiktoken. It also:
    - drops chunks under 30 tokens, as on main;
    - keeps chunk ids in the {sha256}:{i} form;
    - recovers each chunk's position in the document, because the splitter
      returns plain strings.
  - CHUNK_TOKENS still means text only, as on main. This departs from the design
    sketch, which counted the "title · section" header too (about 460). Keeping
    it this way makes the 200/300/400 sweep a like-for-like comparison, and 400
    plus a 10–30-token header still fits under bge's 512.
  - Tests: test_chunking.py moved to test_tenk_parser.py. Every old behaviour is
    still tested, plus tests for both traps, repeated text, and the node's
    embedded text. chunk_stats.py now uses the new parser.
  - Design doc: findings 17–19 added.

  chunk_stats on the corpus: no chunk is over its token limit. The chunks that
  end on an abbreviation all end a real sentence ("…Form 10-K.", "…outside the
  U.S.").

  Eval (one run), compared with main:

  ┌───────────┬───────────┬──────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐

      279 +Found while building stage 5 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      280 +                                                              
      281 +20. **`query(str)` takes only a string.** `/api/ask` passes fi
          +lters and `top_k` per request, and reports timings, token coun
          +ts and the refusal reason. So `GroundedQueryEngine` keeps `ask
          +(question, filters, top_k) -> Answer` as the app's entry point
          +, and `custom_query` wraps it: the `Response` carries the cite
          +d passages as `source_nodes`, in citation order, and the `Answ
          +er` in `metadata["answer"]`.                                  
      282 +21. **`CustomQueryEngine.query` skips instrumentation.** It ov
          +errides `BaseQueryEngine.query` without the `@dispatcher.span`
          + decorator and without the `QueryStartEvent`/`QueryEndEvent` c
          +alls. LlamaIndex's instrumentation therefore sees the engine's
          + retriever and LLM spans, but no query span. This is worth kno
          +wing before stage 6 adds an instrumentation handler.          
      283 +22. **The engine is a pydantic model.** Its fields are type-ch
          +ecked, so the `Retriever` Protocol is now `@runtime_checkable`
          +. Constructor arguments must be keywords.                     

⏺ Stage 5 is committed as b06e816: /api/ask now answers through
  GroundedQueryEngine, a LlamaIndex CustomQueryEngine. Your answer eval is the
  remaining check; I haven't run it.

  What changed
  - qa/service.py: QAService is now GroundedQueryEngine.
    - ask(question, filters, top_k) -> Answer is still what /api/ask and the
      answer eval call. The relevance gate, the refusal marker, the prompt and
      the citation check are unchanged. qa/prompt.py and qa/citations.py are
      untouched.
    - The standard LlamaIndex engine.query("…") also works. It returns a
      Response whose source_nodes are the cited passages in citation order, with
      the full Answer in metadata["answer"].
  - LLM layer: LlamaIndexClient.chat() takes LlamaIndex chat messages directly,
    so our own ChatMessage and LLMClient are gone from llm/base.py. Error
    mapping, the truncation and empty-reply checks, and token counts are
    unchanged.
  - api/ and eval/: only imports and type names changed. Two leftovers I kept to
    stay within the wiring-only rule:
    - The QAServiceDep alias keeps its name, so routes_ask.py didn't need
      touching.
    - The first docstring line of eval/run_answer_eval.py still says "through
      QAService".
  - Tests: all moved to the new names. Three new tests cover the query()
    response, a refusal with no source nodes, and rejecting a retriever without
    retrieve.
  - Design doc: findings 20–22 added. The one that matters next:
    CustomQueryEngine.query emits no query span for LlamaIndex's
    instrumentation. That affects stage 6, which restores the progress bar
    through an instrumentation handler.

  Checks: ruff, format and mypy are clean, and all 339 tests pass. I didn't
  rerun the retrieval eval because retrieval didn't change.

  To run the answer eval: this branch can't read the index built on main (it
  raises IndexFormatError). Point DATA_DIR at a fresh directory, or move
  data/index aside, then from backend/:

  uv run python scripts/ingest.py
  uv run python eval/run_answer_eval.py ask
  uv run python eval/run_answer_eval.py score
  After your results, I'll start stage 6: IngestionPipeline with a docstore, the
  progress bar through instrumentation, and the write lock plus the BM25
  rebuild-and-swap kept.

⏺ Stage 6 is underway; I've found that the pipeline's default cache keeps every
  embedded node in memory and that it writes chunk 0 first, which could leave a
  half-written document after a crash. I'm reading the docstore and embedding
  events before writing any code.

  duplicates_only would mark failed uploads as done before embedding. Checking
  the tests that guard this next.

      281  20. **`query(str)` takes only a string.** `/api/ask` passes fi
           lters and `top_k` per request, and reports timings, token coun
           ts and the refusal reason. So `GroundedQueryEngine` keeps `ask
           (question, filters, top_k) -> Answer` as the app's entry point
           , and `custom_query` wraps it: the `Response` carries the cite
           d passages as `source_nodes`, in citation order, and the `Answ
           er` in `metadata["answer"]`.
      282  21. **`CustomQueryEngine.query` skips instrumentation.** It ov
           errides `BaseQueryEngine.query` without the `@dispatcher.span`
            decorator and without the `QueryStartEvent`/`QueryEndEvent` c
           alls. LlamaIndex's instrumentation therefore sees the engine's
            retriever and LLM spans, but no query span. This is worth kno
           wing before stage 6 adds an instrumentation handler.
      283  22. **The engine is a pydantic model.** Its fields are type-ch
           ecked, so the `Retriever` Protocol is now `@runtime_checkable`
           . Constructor arguments must be keywords.
      284 +                                                              
      285 +Found while building stage 6 (Nov 9, 2026):                   
      286 +                                                              
      287 +23. **`IngestionPipeline` caches by default.** It builds an in
          +-memory `IngestionCache` that keeps every run's nodes, embeddi
          +ngs included, for as long as the pipeline lives, and the uploa
          +d service's pipeline lives as long as the server. The spike se
          +ts `disable_cache=True`; the docstore already skips re-runs.  
      288 +24. **The pipeline writes node 0 first.** It calls `vector_sto
          +re.add` directly, and `ChromaVectorStore.add` writes in order,
          + in batches of 41,665. A crash could then leave a half-written
          + document that `has_document()` reports as indexed. The revers
          +e batching moved from `ChunkStore.add` into `NodeZeroLastChrom
          +aVectorStore`, the store the pipeline writes to.              
      289 +25. **The docstore strategies.**                              
      290 +    - The default, `upserts`, compares a document hash that co
          +vers text and metadata. Our metadata holds `ingested_at`, so t
          +he same file would hash differently on every run, and the pipe
          +line would delete the indexed document and embed it again. `Fi
          +lingDocument.hash` is the sha256 of the file's bytes, which is
          + also its id.                                                 
      291 +    - `duplicates_only` records a document in the docstore *be
          +fore* running the transformations, so a failed embedding block
          +s every retry in that process. With that strategy, both retry 
          +tests fail.                                                   
      292 +    - The docstore is not saved. It is rebuilt from Chroma whe
          +n a pipeline is built: only documents whose node 0 is stored c
          +ount. Chroma stays the single source of truth (D6).           
      293 +    - `store_doc_text=False` keeps the whole filing's text out
          + of the docstore.                                             
      294 +26. **Instrumentation is global.** Handlers sit on the root di
          +spatcher and see every event from every thread: query embeddin
          +gs, other uploads. They also swallow every exception, so a han
          +dler bug is silent. The progress handler therefore:           
      295 +    - routes by an `instrument_tags` tag, a context variable, 
          +so other threads' events carry no tag;                        
      296 +    - logs its own exceptions;                                
      297 +    - gets the total from a `NodesParsedEvent` that `TenKNodeP
          +arser` emits, because LlamaIndex sends no event with the node 
          +count.                                                        
      298 +                                                              
      299 +    Progress now moves per embedding batch: 16 nodes with fast
          +embed, where `main` moved every 64.                           
      300 +27. **The write lock covers embedding.** The pipeline chunks, 
          +embeds and writes in one call, so a second upload now waits fo
          +r the first to finish embedding, where `main` embedded both in
          + parallel and serialized only the write.                      
      301 +28. **`llama_index.core.instrumentation` re-exports without `_
          +_all__`**, so mypy strict flags `get_dispatcher` and `instrume
          +nt_tags`. A mypy override sets `implicit_reexport` for that pa
          +ckage rather than importing `llama_index_instrumentation`, a t
          +ransitive dependency, directly.                               
      302 +                                                              
      303 +Stage 6 check: `scripts/ingest.py` on the corpus gives 1,023 c
          +hunks, as in stage 4, and a second run skips all six files. Th
          +e retrieval eval was not rerun, because chunk content did not 
          +change.

⏺ Stage 6 is committed as 14950a4: files are now indexed through LlamaIndex's
  IngestionPipeline, and uploads report progress again. One behaviour change
  needs your decision (below).

  What changed
  - ingestion/indexing.py (192 lines) replaces pipeline.py (deleted). The
    pipeline chunks with TenKNodeParser, embeds, then writes to Chroma. Its
    docstore skips files that are already indexed.
  - Upload progress now comes from LlamaIndex's instrumentation: an event from
    our parser gives the chunk count, and LlamaIndex's per-batch embedding
    events give the progress. Each event is tagged with its upload, so searches
    or other uploads running at the same time don't move the bar.
  - uploads.py keeps the write lock, the second "already indexed?" check inside
    the lock, and the BM25 rebuild-and-swap.

  Traps found and handled (findings 23–28 in the design doc)
  - Memory: the pipeline's default cache keeps every run's nodes and embeddings
    for the life of the server. I turned it off.
  - Crash safety: the pipeline writes a document's first chunk first, so a crash
    mid-write could leave a half-indexed document that looks complete and is
    never retried. The "first chunk written last" rule now lives in the vector
    store the pipeline writes to.
  - Docstore deduplication:
    - LlamaIndex's document hash includes ingested_at, so re-ingesting the same
      file would delete it and embed it again. The hash is now the file's
      sha256.
    - The duplicates_only strategy records a file before embedding, so a failed
      upload could never be retried. I use upserts. Switching to duplicates_only
      makes both retry tests fail.
    - The docstore isn't saved to disk. It's rebuilt from Chroma when the
      pipeline is built, so Chroma stays the single source of truth (handoff
      decision D6).
  - Instrumentation: LlamaIndex silently swallows errors raised inside event
    handlers, so the progress handler logs its own.

  Decision for you: an upload now holds the write lock while it embeds, because
  the pipeline chunks, embeds and writes in one call. A second upload waits for
  the first to finish; on main they embedded in parallel and only the write was
  serialized. For a single-user demo I think that's acceptable. If you want
  parallel embedding back, the pipeline could embed without the vector store and
  we'd write under the lock ourselves, but the docstore would then do less of
  the work.

  Checks
  - ruff, format and mypy are clean, and all 347 tests pass, including 8 new
    pipeline tests.
  - I added a mypy override in pyproject.toml, because mypy strict rejects how
    LlamaIndex re-exports its instrumentation helpers. No dependency changed and
    uv.lock is unchanged.
  - scripts/ingest.py on the real corpus produced 1,023 chunks, the same as
    stage 4. A second run skipped all six files, and a search on the new index
    returned sensible results.
  - I didn't rerun the retrieval eval, because chunk content didn't change.

  Next is stage 7, docs only: README, HANDOFF D1, IMPROVEMENTS, CLAUDE.md, and
  the "Main vs spike" table (lines of code we own, dependencies added, eval
  numbers side by side). Your answer-eval numbers from stage 5 would complete
  that table. I'll wait for your go-ahead.

         -icate the LLM adapters and hide the citation logic in its templ

         +nd about 200 more lines of our own code, mostly correcting Llam
         +aIndex defaults that do not suit filings |

          oq or Ollama, and tests use a fake | A small interface to maint

          sses. Reading the question and passage together raised R@6 at 4
          00 tokens from 0.83 to 0.92 (HTML) and 0.76 to 0.81 (TXT). Chos

      96  | **Chroma as the source of truth** | Embedded with no operatio

⏺ Stage 7 is committed as c582f3e, and that finishes the spike: eight commits on
  spike/llamaindex, with main untouched. The one result still missing is your
  answer eval on this branch.

  The main finding contradicts the design doc. It predicted that about 700 lines
  would go and about 200 arrive. Measured, the code we own grew from 2,692 to
  2,892 lines (+7%), and tests grew from 3,145 to 3,825.

  ┌──────────────────────────────┬───────┬───────┐
  │             Area             │ main  │ Spike │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ llm/                         │ 289   │ 173   │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ retrieval/                   │ 583   │ 702   │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ ingestion/ (without loaders) │ 516   │ 695   │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ qa/                          │ 222   │ 240   │
  ├──────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┤
  │ All of backend/src           │ 2,692 │ 2,892 │
  └──────────────────────────────┴───────┴───────┘

  Code shrank where LlamaIndex replaced ours outright: the LLM adapters, the
  fusion function, the BM25 wrapper. It grew wherever a LlamaIndex default
  didn't suit filings and had to be corrected: the sentence splitter, scores
  lost in fusion, zero-score BM25 hits, write order, the docstore hash, the
  cache, progress routing. Each correction is small but needs a comment and a
  test. All 28 findings are listed in docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md.

  Retrieval eval: at the default 400 tokens every row is identical to main,
  including the re-ranked rows and the gate table, so all unanswerable questions
  are still refused. At 200 and 300 tokens, recall in the top 6 is also
  identical, and MRR moves by less than one question. Search time is the same
  within noise.

  Dependencies: four direct llama-index-* packages, pinned exactly because some
  of our subclasses call their private methods. openai is downgraded to 2.x and
  PyStemmer kept at 3.1 through an override. The lock gains 28 packages
  (including nltk, tiktoken and sqlalchemy, none called by our code) and loses
  2. I didn't measure the Docker image size.

  What was lost: the Anthropic provider, RRF_K as a setting, parallel embedding
  of uploads, and free upgrades of LlamaIndex.

  Docs updated so a later session doesn't revert the branch:
  - docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md: the full "Main vs spike" section, which holds the
    tables above, the dependency details and the loss list.
  - CLAUDE.md: the rule now says LlamaIndex does the plumbing on this branch and
    points to the traps list.
  - docs/HANDOFF.md: rule 3, the decisions on framework, LLM provider and
    fusion, the dependency list, the module list, the layout tree and the config
    block. The milestone history is unchanged.
  - README.md: the framework decision, the Anthropic row, a line on which
    LlamaIndex components are used, the test count (347), and a note that its
    answer-eval numbers are from main.
  - docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md: the framework rule, plus an open item for your answer
    eval.

  To complete the table, run the answer eval from backend/ with a fresh
  DATA_DIR: scripts/ingest.py, then eval/run_answer_eval.py ask, then score.
  I'll add your numbers to "Main vs spike" next to main's.

  The differences come from the model, not the spike:
  - Same prompts: all 53 questions used the same number of input tokens as on
    main. 51 of them got the same passages in the same order; the other two, u02
    and u03, were refused on both branches.
  - Changed outcomes: four questions changed outcome, each with the same
    passages as on main:
    - m25: main refused it; the spike answered with a computed figure that the
      scorer doesn't accept.
    - m02: the answer said "$4,379 million", but the question's pattern wants
      "$4.38 billion".
    - m04 and a04: cited different filings than on main.
  - Latency: the median per question was 1,502 ms on the spike, against 1,483 ms
    on main.

  I was wrong about why the run was slow: it wasn't slow Groq calls. The log
  shows the eval's own rate limiter waiting about a minute between questions to
  stay under the free tier's limit.

  main's eval/answer_results.md is unchanged. To see the spike's run scored in
  that format, run run_answer_eval.py score --run

✻ Baked for 1m 23s · done 3:43 PM

※ recap: The LlamaIndex spike is finished and in draft PR #5, and its retrieval and answer evals are both within one question of main. Next, you decide whether uploads should keep queuing behind one write lock during embedding.

❯ what can we gain using the llamaindex? Scaling or something else? Because     
  right now I dont see the benefits                                             

⏺ For this app as it stands, LlamaIndex gives us almost nothing, and scaling
  isn't one of the gains. The spike's own numbers show that: answers and
  retrieval match main, the code we own grew from 2,692 to 2,892 lines, and we
  found 28 defaults we had to correct.

  Why it doesn't help with scaling

  LlamaIndex is a thin layer over the parts that limit scale, and those parts
  are the same on both branches:
  - Search: Chroma, embedded in the API process.
  - BM25: LlamaIndex's BM25Retriever is held in memory and rebuilt after each
    upload, exactly like ours.
  - Embedding and the LLM: CPU embedding and Groq's rate limit set the speed on
    both branches.

  Scaling up would mean replacing those parts: a server-backed vector database,
  a search engine with built-in keyword search, an embedding service. That work
  is about the same with or without LlamaIndex.

  What it could give us later

  Each of these depends on a feature we don't have:
  - Swapping the vector database. Changing Chroma for Qdrant or pgvector would
    mostly mean changing the vector-store class. Our search code would barely
    change. This only matters if we actually move off Chroma.
  - Comparison questions across filings, such as "How did Apple's R&D change
    from 2000 to 2026?". Its SubQuestionQueryEngine splits a question into one
    per filing and combines the answers. Our corpus is built around filing
    pairs, so this is the strongest product reason. We could also write it
    ourselves in roughly 100 lines.
  - PDFs and scanned filings, through its parsing service, LlamaParse. That's a
    paid service, and we only ingest TXT and HTML today.
  - Tracing in an observability tool. The instrumentation we already use for
    upload progress can send traces to Langfuse or Arize Phoenix with little
    extra code.
  - Distributed ingestion. IngestionPipeline can run parse and embed workers in
    parallel and keep its dedup records in Redis. This only becomes worthwhile
    when ingesting thousands of filings, not 6.

  What it costs
  - llama-index-core stays pinned exactly, because our subclasses call its
    private methods. Every upgrade means re-checking our corrections.
  - We lost the Anthropic provider and RRF_K as a setting.
  - The lock gained 28 packages.
  - Its defaults need watching. Several of the 28 corrections would have quietly
    hurt answers if the eval hadn't caught them, such as chunks cut after "Mr."
    or "Inc." and table rows split inside numbers like "1,234".

  My recommendation

  Don't merge. I'd turn PR #5 from a draft into a closed record: close it
  without merging and keep the branch and docs/LLAMAINDEX_SPIKE.md as the answer
  to "why not LlamaIndex?".

  Revisit LlamaIndex only if the roadmap adds a second vector database,
  comparison questions across many filings, or PDF ingestion at volume. Even
  then, using just the piece we need is probably better than routing everything
  through it.

  Shall I close the PR, or would you rather keep it open while you decide?
